Chapter VIII— Agricultural Stabiliza­
tion and Conservation S e rv ice  
(Sugar), Department of Agriculture
SUBCHAPTER G— DETERMINATION OF  
PROPORTIONATE SHARES  
[Amdt. 1]
PART 855— MAINLAND CANE SUGAR  
AREA
Proportionate Shares for Farms; 1969  
Crop -
Pursuant to section 302 of the Sugar  
A ct o f 1948, as amended (hereinafter  
referred to as the A ct, §§ 855.49 and  
855.51 (33 F .R . 12027) are amended as  
follow s:
1. Section 855.49 is amended to read  
as follow s:
§ 855.49 State acreage allocations.
The acreage allocation shall be 160,270  
acres for Florida and 256,800 acres for  
Louisiana, which includes the acreage  
made available under § 855.55 for new -  
producer farm s and under § 855.56 for  
fulfilling appeals and correcting errors.
2. Paragraphs (b) and (c) of § 855.51  
are amended to read as follow s:
§ 855.51 Establishment of shares for 
old-producer farms. 
* * * * *
(b) Farms with bases of more than  
50 acres and less than 58.9 acres.  The  
share for any farm  in this category shall 
be 50 acres.
(c) Farms with bases of 58J9 acres or  
more. The share for any fa rm , in this  
category shall be determined by applying  
to each farm  base ah adjustm ent factor 
computed by the State comm ittee. The  
factor shall be determined by dividing the 
State acreage allocation in § 855.49, less  
the acreage made available to the State  
in §§855.55 and 855.56 and the total of  
the shares determined in paragraphs (a)  
and (b) of this section, by the total of  
the bases established for all farm s with  
bases of 58.9 acres or more.
* * * * *
Statement of bases and considerations.  
The regulations establishing 1969-crop  
proportionate shares for farm s in the  
M ainland Cane Sugar Area previously  
issued (33 F .R . 12027), provided for 1969- 
crop State acreage allocations at 80 per­
cent of the 1968-crop levels. Having re­
appraised the situation with respect to  
proportionate shares for 1969-crop sugar  
cane production in the M ainland Cane  
Sugar Area, it is determined that a 1969-  
crop acreage level at 85 percent of the  
1968-crop level will enable the area to  
meet its quota and provide a norm al  
carryover inventory. The regulations  
have been amended accordingly. This  
area is subject to weather hazards which  
severely affect sugar production and this  
change will lessen the impact next year  
that the larger reduction would have had  
on this industry. The change in § 855.51  
from  62.6 acres to 58.9 acres assures ^that 
shares for farm s having bases of more  
than 50 acres and less than 58.9 acres  
will not be below 50 acres which is the  
share determined by applying a reduc­
tion factor o f 85 percent to a base o f 58.8 
a c re s.,
Accordingly, I  hereby find and conclude 
th at the foregoing amendment will e f­
fectuate the applicable provisions o f the 
A ct.
(Sec. 403, 61 Stat. 932; 7 U.S.C. 1153; secs. 301, 
302, 61 Stat. 929, 930, as amended; 7 U.S.C. 
1131, 1132)

tember 27,1968.

THE PRESIDENT 14137
Executive Order 11256
ESTABLISHING THE PRESIDENT’S COMMITTEE ON FOOD AND FIBER AND 
THE NATIONAL ADVISORY COMMISSION ON FOOD AND FIBER
By virtue of the authority vested in me as President of the United 
States, it is ordered as follows :
Section If Establishm ent o f Committee, (a) There is hereby es­
tablished the President’ s Committee on Food and Fiber (hereinafter 
referred to as the “Committee”).
(b) The Committee shall be composed of the Secretary of Agricul­
ture, who shall be the Chairman of the Committee; the Secretary of 
State ; the Secretary of Commerce ; and the Secretary of Labor. Each 
member of the Committee may designate an alternate member, who 
shall serve as a member of the Committee whenever the regular mem­
ber is unable to attend any meeting of the Committee.
S ec. 2. D uties o f the Committee, (a) The Committee shall:
(1) Appraise existing and alternative agricultural policies and for­
eign trade policies related thereto in terms of the national interest, the 
welfare of farmers, workers, consumers, rural Americans, and the 
general public; their effects on the performance of the economy and 
on foreign relations; and their implications for the optimum alloca­
tion of Federal resources among national objectives; and
(2) Develop such recommendations for action by Government or 
by private enterprise as it deems most appropriate to advance the 
general public interest.
(b) Such recommendations shalltake into account the final report 
of the National Advisory Commission on Food and Fiber established 
by Section 3 of this order, which report shall be transmitted to the 
President with the recommendations of the Committee.
S ec. 3. E stablishm ent o f Commission. (a) To assist the Com­
mittee in carrying out its functions under Section 2 of this order, 
there is hereby established the National Advisory Commission on 
Food and Fiber (hereinafter referred to as the “Commission”).
(b) The Commission shall be composed of members appointed by 
the President. The President shall designate the Chairman of the 
Commission from among its members.
(c) The Commission shall meet on call of the Chairman.
(d) Each member of the Commission may be compensated for each 
day such member is engaged upon the work of the Commission, and 
shall be reimbursed for travel expenses, including per diem in lieu of 
subsistence, as authorized by law (5 U.S.C. 55a; 5 U.S.C. 73b-2) for 
persons in the Government service employed intermittently.
(e) The Commission shall have an Executive Director, who shall 
be designated by the President. Under the direction and supervision 
of the Chairman, the Executive Director shall direct and supervise 
staff assigned to assist the Commission in carrying out its responsibili­
ties under this order.
S ec. 4. D uties o f the Commission,  (a) The Commission shall 
transmit to the Committee its independent analysis, evaluation, and 
recommendations with respect to all matters assigned to the Commit­
tee for study and recommendations.
(b) In  carrying out its duties under subsection (a) above, the 
Commission shall :
FEDERAL REGISTER, VOL. 30, NO. 218— WEDNESDAY, NOVEMBER 10, 1965

=== PDF page 8 of 64 ===
14138 THE PRESIDENT
( 1 ) Make a comprehensive study and appraisal of the "current 
economic situation and trends in American farming, including pro­
ductivity, costs, prices, incomes, farm employment, labor standards, 
foreign trade, and related matters ;
(2) Evaluate existing and alternative agricultural policies and for­
eign trade policies related thereto in terms of the national interest, 
the welfare of farmers, workers, consumers, rural Americans, and 
the general public; their effects on the performance of the economy 
and on foreign relations ; and their implications for the optimum 
allocation of Federal resources among national objectives; and
(3) Develop such recommendations for action by Government or 
private enterprise as it deems appropriate to advance the general 
public interest.
(c) The Commission shall submit its final report and recommenda­
tions to the Committee no later than eighteen months after the date 
of its first meeting,' and may make any interim reports which it deems 
would expedite the work of the Committee.
S ec. 5. Federal departments and agencies,  (a) The Committee 
or the Commission is authorized to request from any Federal depart­
ment, agency, or independent instrumentality of the Government any 
information deemed necessary to carry out its functions under this 
order ; and each department, agency, and instrumentality is authorized, 
to the extent permitted by law and within the limits of available funds, 
to furnish such information to the Committee or the Commission.
(b) Each department the head of which is named in Section 1(b) 
of this order shall, as may be necessary, furnish assistance to the Com­
mittee or the Commission in accordance with the provisions of Section 
214 of the Act of May 3, 1945 (59 Stat. 134; 31 TJ.S.C. 691), or as 
otherwise permitted by law.
(c) The General Services Administration is hereby designated as 
the agency which shall, on a reimbursable basis, provide administrative 
services for the Commission.
S ec. 6. Term ination o f the Committee and the Commission.  The 
Committee and the Commission shall terminate ninety days after the 
submission of the final report of the Commission.
S ec. 7. Revocation o f orders. Executive Order 10472 of July 20, 
1953, and Executive Order 10937 of May 3, 1961, are hereby revoked.
T he  W hite  H ouse,
November  J, 1965.
L yndon  B. J ohnson
[F.R. Doc. 65-12155; Filed, Nov. 9, 1965; 9:39  a.m.]
FEDERAL REGISTER, VOL. 30, NO. 218— WEDNESDAY, NOVEMBER 10, 1965

Federal Register  
Vol. 48, No. 3
Wednesday, January 5, 1983
Presidential Documents
Title 3—
The President
Executive Order 12398 of December 31, 1982
President's Private Sector Survey on Cost Control in the  
Federal Government
By the authority vested in me as President by the Constitution and laws of the  
United States of America, including the Federal Advisory Committee Act, as  
amended (5 U.S.C. App. I), and in order to increase the membership and  
extend the life of the President’s Private Sector Survey on Cost Control in the  
Federal Government, it is hereby ordered that Executive Order No. 12369 of  
June 30,1982, is amended as follows:
(a) The second sentence of Section 1(a) shall read: “The Committee shall be  
composed of not more than 170 members appointed by the President from  
among citizens in private life.’’.
(b) Section 4(b) shall read: “In accordance with the Federal Advisory Commit­
tee Act, as amended, the Committee shall terminate on June 30, 1983, unless  
sooner extended.”.
THE WHITE HOUSE,  
D ecem b er 31, 1982.
[FR Doc. 83-384]
Filed 1-3-83; 4:25 pm]  
Billing code 3195-01-M

THE PRESIDENT 3811
Executive Order 11329
CREATING A BOARD OF INQUIRY TO REPORT ON A LABOR DISPUTE
AFFECTING THE SHIPBUILDING AND REPAIR INDUSTRIES OF THE
UNITED STATES
W H E R E A S , there exists a labor dispute between the Pacific Coast 
Shipbuilders’ Association, Alameda, California, representing Lock­
heed Shipbuilding and Construction Company, Seattle, Washington,  
Bethlehem Steel Company, San Francisco, California, Lake Union  
Dry Docks Company, Seattle, Washington, Pacific Coast Engineering  
Company, Alameda, California, Albina Engine and Machine Works,  
Portland, Oregon, Northwest Marine Iron Works, Portland, Oregon,  
Todd Shipyards Corporation, Seattle, Washington and Alameda,  
California, Guy F. Atkinson Company, Portland, Oregon, Willamette  
Iron and Steel Company, Portland, Oregon, and certain of their em­
ployees represented by the International Brotherhood of Electrical  
Workers, A F L -C I O ; and
W H E R E A S , such dispute has resulted in a strike which will, in my 
opinion, if permitted to continue, affect a substantial part of the ship­
building and repair industries, which industries are engaged in trade,  
commerce, transportation, transmission, or communication among the  
several States or with foreign nations, or engaged m the production of  
goods for commerce, and which strike, if permitted to continue, will  
imperil the national safety:
N O W  T H E R E F O R E , by virtue of the authority vested in me by 
Section 206 of the Labor-Management Relations Act of 1947 (61 Stat.  
155: 29 U.S.C. 176),H  hereby create a Board of Inquiry, consisting of  
Mr. J. Keith Mann, Chairman, Mr. George E. Reedy and Mr. Paul D.  
Hanlon, whom I  appoint to inquire into the issues involved m this
dispute.
The Board shall have powers and duties as set forth in Title I I  of  
such Act. The Board shall report to the President m accordance  
with the provisions of Section 206 of such Act on or before March 8,  
1967.
Upon submission of its report, the Board shall continue in existence  
to perform such other functions as may be required under such Act.
T h e W  h it e H ouse ,
March 2,1967.
10:46 p.m. Thursday Night. En Route Texas, Airforce I.
[F.R. Doc. 67-2629; Filed, Mar. 6,1967; 4:10 p.m.]
FEDERAL REGISTER, VOL. 32, NO. 45— WEDNESDAY, MARCH 8, 1967

=== PDF page 11 of 52 ===
THE PRESIDENT 13419
Executive Order No. 11314
CREATING A BOARD OF INQUIRY TO REPORT ON CERTAIN LABOR
DISPUTES AFFECTING THE MILITARY JET ENGINE INDUSTRY, MILI­
TARY AIRCRAFT INDUSTRY, MILITARY ARMAMENT INDUSTRY AND
MILITARY ELECTRONICS INDUSTRY OF THE UNITED STATES
W H E R E A S, there have existed certain labor disputes between Gen­
eral Electric Company and certain of its employees represented by : 
International Union of Electrical Radio and Machine Workers, 
A F L -C IO ; International Association of Machinists and Aerospace 
Workers, A F L -C IO ; International Union, United Automobile, Aero­
space and A gricultural Implement Workers of America, A F L -C IO  ; 
International Union, Allied Industrial W orkers of America, A F L - 
CIO ; International Brotherhood of Electrical Workers, A F L -C IO ; 
Sheet Metal W orkers’  International Association, A F L -C IO ; Ameri­
can Federation of Technical Engineers, A F L -C IO  ; American F lint 
Glass W orkers’  Union of North America, A F L -C IO ; United 
Electrical Radio and Machine W orkers of America; United Associa­
tion of Journeymen and Apprentices of the Plumbing and Pipe F itting 
Industry of the United States and Canada, A F L -C IO  ; United Steel­
workers of America, A F L -C IO ; International Union of Operating 
Engineers, A F L -C IO  ; International Brotherhood of Teamsters, 
Chauffeurs, Warehousemen and Helpers of America ; Pattern Makers’  
League of North America, A F L -C IO  ; International Brotherhood of 
Firemen and Oilers, A F L -C IO ; Syracuse Draftsmens Union; and
W H E R E A S, although a tentative resolution of these disputes was 
arrived at by the parties, or some of them, nevertheless there is a strike 
at the Evendale, Ohio, plant of General Electric Company by Local 
No. 647 of the International Union of the United Automobile, Aero­
space and Agricultural Implement Workers of America, A F L -C IO , 
and Locals 34 and 912 of the International Association of Machinists 
and Aerospace Workers, A F L -C IO , and there exists a threat that 
there will be further strikes at General Electric Company plants by 
one or more of the aforesaid international unions or local units 
thereof ; and
W H E R E A S, the existing strike at the Evendale, Ohio, plant of 
General Electric Company will, in my opinion, if permitted to con­
tinue, affect a substantial p art of the m ilitary jet engine industry, 
which industry is engaged in trade, commerce, transportation, trans­
mission, or communication among the several states or with foreign 
nations, or engaged in the production of goods for commerce, and which 
strike, if permitted to continue, will imperil the national safety ; and
W H E R E A S, threatened strikes at other General Electric Company 
plants will, in my opinion, if allowed to occur, affect a substantial 
p art of the military jet engine industry, m ilitary aircraft industry, 
m ilitary armament industry and m ilitary electronics industry, which 
industries are engaged in trade, commerce, transportation, transmis­
sion, or communication among the several states or with foreign na­
tions, or engaged in the production of goods for commerce, and which 
threatened strikes, if permitted to occur, will imperil the national 
safety ;
NOW T H E R E F O R E  by virtue of the authority vested in me by 
Section 206 of the Labor Management Relations Act of 1947 (61 Stat. 
155; 29 U.S.C. 176), I  hereby create a Board of Inquiry, consisting 
of Mr. David L. Cole, Chairman, Mr. John Dunlop and Mr. Jacob 
Seidenberg, whom I  appoint to inquire into the issues involved in 
these disputes.
FEDERAL REGISTER, VOL. 31, NO. 202— TUESDAY, OCTOBER 18, 1966

=== PDF page 12 of 52 ===
13420 THE PRESIDENT
The Board shall have powers and duties as set forth in Title I I  of 
such Act. The Board shall report to the President in accordance 
with the provisions of Section 206 of such Act. W ith respect to the 
dispute giving rise to the strike at Evendale, Ohio, plant it shall report 
on or before October 18th, 1966, and with respect to the dispute or 
disputes resulting in any other such strike, it shall report as soon as 
possible.
Upon the submission of its report or reports, the Board shall con­
tinue in existence to perform such other functions as may be required 
under such Act.
L yndon  B. J ohnson
T h e W h it e H ouse ,
October 17 ,  1966.
[F.R. Doc. 66-11415; Filed, Oct. IT, 1966; 1:07 p.m.]
FEDERAL REGISTER, VOL. 31, NO. 202— TUESDAY, OCTOBER 18, 1966

```

Federal Register /  Vol. 48, No. 211 / Monday, October 31, 1983 /  Presidential Documents 50055
Presidential Documents
Executive Order 12447 of October 27, 1983
President’ s Private Sector Survey on Cost Control in the 
Federal Government
By the authority vested in me as President by the Constitution and laws of the  
United States of America, including the Federal Advisory Committee Act, as  
amended (5 U.S.C. App. I), and in order to extend the life of the President’s  
Private Sector Survey on Cost Control in the Federal Government, it is hereby  
ordered that Section 4(b) of Executive Order No. 12369 of June 30, 1982, as  
amended, is further amended to read:
“The Committee shall terminate on December 31, 1983, unless sooner ex­
tended.”.
THE WHITE HOUSE, 
O c to b e r 27, 1983.[FR Doc. 83-29686 
Filed 10-28-83; 11:20 am] 
Billing code 3195-01-M

I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11314.txt, 31 FR 13419, PDF pages 11-12). The dossier's inventory is incomplete or split mid-sentence (it omits the order's opening paragraphs or divides the report date from its year), so I replaced it with one clause covering the whole printed order from its heading to its last sentence. EO 12553 (signed 1986-02-25) revokes the order by number. The first end is that express revocation. The board's report date and any later discharge of the board are not established by a held reviewed source and are not ends. Bounded search read for this order: the dossier's three machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121 through 2026-10-09; eCFR and GovInfo CFR bulk through 2026-10-14; Federal Register catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of 17,521 saved order texts), after reading their coverage notes, which carry no coverage warning; the Cruden by-number scan of held order texts in the packet's LOOKUPS.md; and a full read of EO 12553 (held FR text, 33,227 characters).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: the order sets a report date ('report on or before October 18th, 1966 for the Evendale dispute and as soon as possible for any other strike') and says the board continues in existence after reporting to perform functions required under the Act; it states no end date. A report date is a deadline, not an end. The board's actual report, any settlement and any discharge of the board under Title II of the Act are not established by a held reviewed source, so no earlier end is claimed. The by-number scan lists no held later order citing this order other than EO 12553, which names it; the catalog note, the U.S. Code and the eCFR searches add nothing earlier.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order creates the board, appoints its named members and sets its report date. The appointments are one-time acts whose surviving consequence (the members' service and the board's report) is a completed fact; no held source shows when the board was discharged, so no earlier end is asserted. The order creates no fund, delegation or continuing authority outside the board and survives only as long as the order does.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: none in EO 12553 or in this order; the board's acts are completed facts and nothing continues independently of the order.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole-order scope: EO 12553 revokes the order in full; its table entry carries no 'in part' limit, so the single corrected clause ends on the revocation date. I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11329.txt, 32 FR 3811, PDF pages 7-7). The dossier's inventory is incomplete or split mid-sentence (it omits the order's opening paragraphs or divides the report date from its year), so I replaced it with one clause covering the whole printed order from its heading to its last sentence. EO 12553 (signed 1986-02-25) revokes the order by number. The first end is that express revocation. The board's report date and any later discharge of the board are not established by a held reviewed source and are not ends. Bounded search read for this order: the dossier's three machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121 through 2026-10-09; eCFR and GovInfo CFR bulk through 2026-10-14; Federal Register catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of 17,521 saved order texts), after reading their coverage notes, which carry no coverage warning; the Cruden by-number scan of held order texts in the packet's LOOKUPS.md; and a full read of EO 12553 (held FR text, 33,227 characters).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: the order sets a report date ('report on or before March 8, 1967') and says the board continues in existence after reporting to perform functions required under the Act; it states no end date. A report date is a deadline, not an end. The board's actual report, any settlement and any discharge of the board under Title II of the Act are not established by a held reviewed source, so no earlier end is claimed. The by-number scan lists no held later order citing this order other than EO 12553, which names it; the catalog note, the U.S. Code and the eCFR searches add nothing earlier.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order creates the board, appoints its named members and sets its report date. The appointments are one-time acts whose surviving consequence (the members' service and the board's report) is a completed fact; no held source shows when the board was discharged, so no earlier end is asserted. The order creates no fund, delegation or continuing authority outside the board and survives only as long as the order does.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: none in EO 12553 or in this order; the board's acts are completed facts and nothing continues independently of the order.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole-order scope: EO 12553 revokes the order in full; its table entry carries no 'in part' limit, so the single corrected clause ends on the revocation date. No held source records a partial amendment.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:13","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:13","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11329#corrected-1"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"a9cb9f102d137df2cb8136ef5168fa25e80f906e5ab7bc908184b7a5fb2ece43","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11329.txt","sha256":"a9cb9f102d137df2cb8136ef5168fa25e80f906e5ab7bc908184b7a5fb2ece43","state":"match"},"sha256":"ff19cbe96323d2fc42341ba19254ef3b48e308aa7fc12630c24838dc416a0f3c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11329.txt","text_sha256":"a9cb9f102d137df2cb8136ef5168fa25e80f906e5ab7bc908184b7a5fb2ece43"}]}]}

I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11343.txt, 32 FR 6085, PDF pages 5-5) and confirmed that its printed number, title and signature date match the census register row. The order has one operative paragraph with no numbered sections and no stated end date, so the dossier's single whole-order clause is the complete inventory (any carrier list the order attaches is an incorporated list, not a separate command). EO 12553 (signed 1986-02-25; held text jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt) revokes the order by number in its table. The first end is that express revocation. The report deadline, the report itself and the end of the standstill are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and no held reviewed source establishes the dates on which the board reported or the standstill ran out. Bounded search read for this order: the dossier's three machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121 through 2026-10-09; eCFR and GovInfo CFR bulk through 2026-10-14; Federal Register catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of 17,521 saved order texts), after reading their coverage notes, which carry no coverage warning; the Cruden by-number scan of held order texts in the packet's LOOKUPS.md; and a full read of EO 12553 (held FR text, 33,227 characters).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: the order states a report deadline (\\\"within 30 days from the date of this order\\\") and a standstill running (\\\"for thirty days after the board has made its report\\\"). A report deadline is a deadline, not an end, and the standstill is a spent fact whose date no held source fixes; neither is recorded as an end. The order states no date on which it ends. Held later instruments: the by-number scan lists no held later order that cites this order other than EO 12553, which names it in its revocation table; the Federal Register catalog note records the EO 12553 revocation and the U.S. Code and eCFR searches return no hit. No unnamed statute or order was found that would be an earlier candidate end beyond section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, the statute the order itself invokes.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order (1) creates the board and bars members with a financial or organizational interest, (2) directs a report within thirty days and (3) recites the section 10 standstill. Command (3) rests on the statute, which binds the parties for its own thirty-day period regardless of the order, so it is not an independent surviving command of the order. The order makes no appointment by name, creates no fund or property right and delegates no continuing authority, so nothing in it outlives its revocation.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: no savings language in EO 12553 or in this order; the completed facts are spent and nothing continues independently.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole-order scope: EO 12553 revokes the order in full. Its table lists the order by number with no 'in part' or section limitation, so the single clause ends on the revocation date, and the held sources record no partial amendment of this order.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:14","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:14","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:11343#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:11343#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"e52c343b97e687b9779972a619e323d1e7d0a78d4f25187330618cdc8cedd814","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11343.txt","sha256":"e52c343b97e687b9779972a619e323d1e7d0a78d4f25187330618cdc8cedd814","state":"match"},"sha256":"e73f3bb6b7fefb2ce98accf85b5e4dd461650cf209f3745651ba85290a079f76","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11343.txt","text_sha256":"e52c343b97e687b9779972a619e323d1e7d0a78d4f25187330618cdc8cedd814"}]}]}

- **ACT:EO:11355>EO:12919**; S538c51350de60909faece2f4; {"actor_id":"EO:12919","char_end":35560,"char_start":35076,"confidence":"medium","effective_class":"unknown","effective_date":null,"effective_wording":[{"char_end":4626,"char_start":3802,"text":"=== PDF page 186 of 204 ===\n29526 Federal Register / Vol. 59, No. 108 / Tuesday, June 7, 1994 / Presidential Documents\n(2.) Provide for the central coordination of the plans and programs *  \nincident to authorities and functions delegated under this order,  \nand provide guidance and procedures approved by the Assistant  \nto the President for National Security Affairs to the Federal depart­\nments and agencies under this order;\n(3) Establish procedures» in consultation with Federal departments  \nand agencies assigned functions under this order, to résolve in  \na. timely and effective manner conflicts and issues that may arise  \nin implementing the authorities and functions delegated under this  \norder; and\n(4) Report to the President periodically concerning all program activi­\nties conducted pursuant to this order."},{"char_end":19388,"char_start":19207,"text":"The heads of  \nFederal departments and agencies shall provide the Secretary of Commerce  \nwith such information as may be necessary for the effective performance  \nof this function."},{"char_end":23013,"char_start":22634,"text":"(g) This order suspends any delegated authority, regulation, or other re­\nquirement or condition with respect to the activation of any NDER unit, \nin whole or in part, or appointment of any NDER member that is inconsistent \nwith the authorities delegated herein, provided that the aforesaid suspension \napplies only as long as sections 703(a) an(K710(e) of the Act are in effect."},{"char_end":25404,"char_start":24067,"text":"=== PDF page 191 of 204 ===\nFederal Register / Vol. 59, No. 108 / Tuesday, June 7, 1994 / Presidential Documents 29531\nactions on labor supply and utilization, (2) the relation of labor supply  \nto materials and facilities requirements, and (3) such other matters as will  \nassist in making the exercise of priority and allocations functions consistent  \nwith effective utilization and distribution of labor;\n(c) Formulate plans, programs, and policies for meeting defense and essen­\ntial civilian labor requirements;\n(d) Project skill shortages to facilitate meeting defense and essential civilian \nneeds and establish training programs;\n(e) Determine the occupations and skills critical to meeting the labor  \nrequirements of defense and essential civilian activities and, with the assist­\nance of the Secretary of Defense, the Director of Selective Service, and  \nsuch other persons as the Director, FEMA, may designate, develop policies  \nregulating the induction and deferment of personnel for the armed services,  \nexcept for civilian personnel in the reserves; and\n(f) Administer an effective labor-management relations policy to support \nthe activities and programs under this order with the cooperation of other \nFederal agencies, including the National Labor Relations Board and the \nFederal Mediation and Conciliation Service."},{"char_end":27548,"char_start":27285,"text":"(d) The Secretary of Defense shall report to Congress on a strategic plan  \nfor developing a cost-effective, comprehensive information system capable  \nof identifying on a timely, ongoing basis vulnerability in critical components  \nand critical technology items."},{"char_end":34922,"char_start":34625,"text":"A u thority,  All previously issued orders, regulations, rulings, certifi­\ncates, directives, and other actions relating to any function affected by this  \norder shall remain in effect except as they are inconsistent with this order  \nor are subsequently amended or revoked under proper authority."}],"exceptions_text":[],"filing_date":null,"id":"ACT:EO:11355>EO:12919","names_target":true,"notes":"Machine lead only; I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11355.txt, 32 FR 7803, PDF pages 7-7) and confirmed its number, title and date against the census register; the three numbered clauses are the complete inventory, and the opening authority paragraph is a lead-in, not a command. EO 12919 (signed 1994-06-03) supersedes or revokes EO 10647 and EO 11355 by number in section 904(a), and the dossier carries the ACT record for it. The first end of every clause is that express revocation. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on strike-and-insert amendments each amending clause continues, amended, until the parent order ends; EO 10647 ended by the same section on the same date. EO 12107 (signed 1978-12-28) later substituted Office of Personnel Management words in EO 11355 and is recorded as a later event of clause 1, not an end. Bounded search read for this order: the dossier's three machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121 through 2026-10-09; eCFR and GovInfo CFR bulk through 2026-10-14; Federal Register catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of 17,521 saved order texts), after reading their coverage notes, which carry no coverage warning; the Cruden by-number scan of held order texts in the packet's LOOKUPS.md; and a full read of EO 12553 (held FR text, 33,227 characters).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: none; the order has no sunset or condition. The only later instruments held that touch this order are EO 12107 (an amendment by word substitution effective 1979-01-01, recorded as a later event) and EO 12919 (the revocation). The Federal Register catalog note on EO 11355 lists exactly those two. No held source shows EO 10647 or the Defense Production Act appointment authority ending before 1994-06-03; the amended sections' own ending is therefore not shown earlier.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: clauses 1 and 2 only rewrite subsections of EO 10647, and clause 3 revokes a Part of EO 10647; the order creates no appointment, fund or delegation of its own. EO 10647 itself ended by the same section of EO 12919, so no command survives apart from the order.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: section 903 of EO 12919 preserves the validity of things done under earlier assignments of authority; the amended commands themselves are revoked by name.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole-order scope: EO 12919 section 904(a) lists EO 11355 in full, without an 'in part' limit, so all three clauses end on the same date. EO 12107 amends clause 1 only by substitution and does not end any I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11431.txt, 33 FR 14697, PDF pages 7-9). The dossier's inventory is incomplete or split mid-sentence (it omits the order's opening paragraphs or divides the report date from its year), so I replaced it with one clause covering the whole printed order from its heading to its last sentence. EO 12553 (signed 1986-02-25) revokes the order by number. The first end is that express revocation. The board's report date and any later discharge of the board are not established by a held reviewed source and are not ends. Bounded search read for this order: the dossier's three machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121 through 2026-10-09; eCFR and GovInfo CFR bulk through 2026-10-14; Federal Register catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of 17,521 saved order texts), after reading their coverage notes, which carry no coverage warning; the Cruden by-number scan of held order texts in the packet's LOOKUPS.md; and a full read of EO 12553 (held FR text, 33,227 characters).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: the order sets a report date ('report on or before October 2, 1968') and says the board continues in existence after reporting to perform functions required under the Act; it states no end date. A report date is a deadline, not an end. The board's actual report, any settlement and any discharge of the board under Title II of the Act are not established by a held reviewed source, so no earlier end is claimed. The by-number scan lists no held later order citing this order other than EO 12553, which names it; the catalog note, the U.S. Code and the eCFR searches add nothing earlier.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order creates the board, appoints its named members and sets its report date. The appointments are one-time acts whose surviving consequence (the members' service and the board's report) is a completed fact; no held source shows when the board was discharged, so no earlier end is asserted. The order creates no fund, delegation or continuing authority outside the board and survives only as long as the order does.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: none in EO 12553 or in this order; the board's acts are completed facts and nothing continues independently of the order.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole-order scope: EO 12553 revokes the order in full; its table entry carries no 'in part' limit, so the single corrected clause ends on the revocation date. No held source records a partial amendment.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:17","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"55551fd7e9cfcbf6f6c8af1c0795595628a981b72c538f5c09769427f86db049","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g08/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:17","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11431#corrected-1"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"14800abd85c56af534ed78f92c7025ce9d97c43d280cb4515a8b58fdecdcd827","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11431.txt","sha256":"14800abd85c56af534ed78f92c7025ce9d97c43d280cb4515a8b58fdecdcd827","state":"match"},"sha256":"b318314f7fb4adcbc5e080b1f593ebbfd0ef7e3c879f84107112a648faefcd39","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11431.txt","text_sha256":"14800abd85c56af534ed78f92c7025ce9d97c43d280cb4515a8b58fdecdcd827"}]}]}

EO 12534 (1985-09-30) revokes the parent EO 12369 without naming EO 12398. Machine searches read: U.S. Code 0 hits, eCFR 0 hits, Federal Register catalog hits not decisive. 59 FR 2935) was read in the held Federal Register text jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12891.txt (PDF pages 21-29 of the capture; the order occupies the first pages and later pages belong to neighbouring documents). The dossier evidence ACT:EO:12891>EO:13062 was re-read against the held EO 13062 text: section 3(a) revokes EO 12891 by number and subject, and section 7 gives the express effective date 1997-09-30. The 18 clauses in the inventory were compared with the order’s sections 1 to 4 and cover each subsection and item. Machine searches read: U.S. Code 0 hits, eCFR 0 hits, Federal Register catalog hits not decisive. The LOOKUPS number search finds EO 13062 and its source-ladder copy as the only other texts citing EO 12891. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, section 4(b) (termination 30 days after the final report) is report-relative: the final report date is not held and the express revocation is the end, with the recital in EO 13062 that its listed committees have terminated and completed their work disclosed.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidates tested for an earlier end: section 4(b) ends the Committee 30 days after its final report to the Working Group. That is a report-relative termination whose trigger date is not held in the dossier, so no exact earlier end date is supported; under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 it is disclosed as a spent fact and the end is the express revocation. EO 13062 recites that its listed committees have terminated, without a date. No other held order amends, revokes or names EO 12891.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Every clause concerns the Advisory Committee’s existence, definitions, functions, administration or termination, and none sets a duty that outlives the Committee; section 4(c) creates no right. All end with the order on revocation.\",\"savings_check\":\"Read for savings: EO 13062 section 3 has no savings language. Accrued: Committee work product, recommendations and funding provided.\",\"scope_check\":\"The inventory covers sections 1 to 4 of the order (18 clauses). No partial amendment, suspension or revocation of any clause is held; EO 13062 revokes the whole Preparation attestations never approve legal conclusions or replace whole-case review.
